Confirmed in the source. Iâ€™m removing the invalid cross-login ticket-reuse policy and adding a regression test for the corrected behavior.

You need to make sure they're not left where a scavenging or spec_janitor mob can get them.  It's worth doing when funds are low and they cost money, or are otherwise difficult to reobtain.

Levels of objects are largely determined by the level of the previous mob reset before them (usually this is the mob carrying or using the item).  Read the area.txt file for details, or db.c for the relevant code.

[2026-07-26 5:36:10 PM NZST] CODEX COMMENTARY

[2026-07-26 5:41:09 PM NZST] CODEX COMMENTARY
The runtime path confirms your rule and adds two important details. A mobile is fuzzed around its source level, the reset subtracts two, and the carried/equipped object gets another `number_fuzzy` pass; Mud School then explicitly forces low-level mob loot to level 1. Room objects inherit the active preceding mobile level, explicit `I` resets use their stated level, and contained objects inherit the container's loaded level. I am representing that as a reset-derived load range alongside the prototype field, so the catalog can stop mistaking cost-era numbers such as 2000 and 2500 for usable item levels.

[2026-07-26 7:44:55 PM NZST] USER
Can you investigate what happens to the weight of objects placed in containers (like the sack)?
[2026-07-26 7:44:55 PM NZST] CODEX COMMENTARY
Yes. I’ve checked the current DD4 source: containers do not reduce carried weight. get_obj_weight() starts with the container’s own weight and recursively adds the full weight of every contained object, so putting 20 lb into Aeloria’s 50 lb large sack produces 70 lb of carried weight, not 50 or 20. I’m checking the transfer bookkeeping too, to confirm there is no separate carry-weight exception when an item moves into a carried container.
[2026-07-26 7:45:32 PM NZST] USER
Bummer.
[2026-07-26 7:45:32 PM NZST] CODEX COMMENTARY
Quite. The one consolation is that containers reduce the top-level carried item count because nested objects are not added back to carry_number; they just provide no weight discount at all. For Aeloria, the 50 lb sack is therefore useful only when item-count pressure matters more than weight pressure, which is emphatically not the case right now.

[2026-07-26 9:32:50 PM NZST] CODEX FINAL
**Recommended Goal**

> Extend DD4Tester into a character-independent HERO Autonomy Engine that accepts a declarative character request (sex, race, class, subclass, and optional personality), creates the character, and autonomously advances it from creation to level 100. The engine must use persistent planning, DD4 source and live evidence, reusable level-band policies, class-aware skill and stat development, equipment and economy management, reboot awareness, death recovery, and human-readable progress commentary. It must require no manual gameplay and contain no character-name-specific behavior.

**Acceptance Criteria**

- Provide one command such as `python -m dd4tester hero --race human --sex female --class mage`.
- Validate every legal race, sex, class, and subclass combination from DD4 data rather than hard-coded character profiles.
- Build policy coverage for levels 0-100, including trainer transitions, prerequisites, practices, stats, combat rotations, equipment modes, provisions, money loops, areas, and withdrawal rules.
- When policy evidence is missing, collect bounded live evidence, record it, and promote only verified reusable policies.
- Resume safely after disconnects, reboots, deaths, empty areas, failed purchases, and process restarts.
- Produce structured decisions, safety explanations, progress summaries, experience commentary, and a final HERO report.
- Prove generality by taking at least one new character from every base class to HERO, using multiple races and both sexes, while running creation-through-level-10 validation for every legal race/class pairing.
- Drive the same autonomy engine through direct Telnet and a Mudlet adapter, with at least one complete visible Mudlet-in-VM HERO run.
- Keep the normal automated test suite offline and add regression tests for every discovered failure.

This is the right next goal because it turns the level-10 matrix proof into a reusable product. It does not mistake taking one existing mage to level 100 for evidence that arbitrary future characters will work.

Implemented. The practice system now has source-backed leveling analyses for all nine base classes: mage, cleric, thief, warrior, psionic, shifter, brawler, ranger, and smithy.

Each priority records its practice type, target proficiency, leveling role, rationale, prerequisites/source references, and whether the current deterministic bot can use it. The live planner intersects that analysis with the trainer's actual practice list, current proficiency, physical/intellectual practice balances, prerequisite progress, per-level spending limits, and remembered trainer rejections.

You can inspect any class with:

python -m dd4tester skill-analysis --class thief
python -m dd4tester skill-analysis --class mage

The class data lives in dd4tester/data/training_priorities.json, planner behavior is in dd4tester/training.py, and README.md documents the command.
